> Engineering evaluations are initiated whenever equipment or system failures are discovered that could have serious effects on safety and operability, and should also be considered when the potential for such failures is suspected. These actual or potential failures could be identified during normal facility operations. Additionally, they could be identified as a result of accidents; operations or maintenance surveillances; preventive or corrective maintenance actions; predictive maintenance monitoring; reviews of industry experience; and adverse conditions or serious failures of similar equipment in other systems or facilities.

## DOC-0032-B02791

Chapter: 300 IDENTIFICATION OF SAMPLES · lines 2792–2792

Criteria: APP_B_VIII — Identification and Control of Materials, Parts, and Components; APP_B_XI — Test Control; APP_B_XIII — Handling, Storage, and Shipping; APP_B_XV — Nonconforming Materials, Parts, or Components

> Samples should be identified in a manner consistent with their intended use. Identification should be maintained throughout acquisition, handling, testing and analysis, preservation, shipment, transfer, storage, and disposition of samples.

## DOC-0032-B02793

Chapter: 300 IDENTIFICATION OF SAMPLES · lines 2794–2794

Criteria: APP_B_V — Instructions, Procedures, and Drawings; APP_B_VI — Document Control; APP_B_VIII — Identification and Control of Materials, Parts, and Components; APP_B_XI — Test Control; APP_B_XIV — Inspection, Test, and Operating Status

> Samples should be identified by placing the identification directly on the samples when possible, or on their container, or on a label or tag attached to the sample or their container. Sample identification should be verified and documented prior to release for testing or analysis. Identification methods should not affect sample characteristics or interface with the intended use.

## DOC-0032-B02795

Chapter: 300 IDENTIFICATION OF SAMPLES · lines 2796–2796

Criteria: APP_B_VI — Document Control; APP_B_VIII — Identification and Control of Materials, Parts, and Components; APP_B_XV — Nonconforming Materials, Parts, or Components

> Identification systems should ensure documented traceability of samples from the initial source, through final disposition. Samples that have lost their identification should not be used for input into the site investigation database.

## DOC-0032-B02799

Chapter: 400 CONTROL OF SAMPLES · lines 2800–2800

Criteria: APP_B_I — Organization; APP_B_VI — Document Control; APP_B_XIII — Handling, Storage, and Shipping; APP_B_XV — Nonconforming Materials, Parts, or Components

> Samples should be controlled during handling, acquisition, transfer of custody, shipment, storage, and disposition to preclude damage and loss (including loss of identity or associated documentation) and minimize deterioration. Responsibilities for control of samples should be defined.

## DOC-0032-B02805

Chapter: 400 CONTROL OF SAMPLES · lines 2806–2806

Criteria: APP_B_I — Organization; APP_B_V — Instructions, Procedures, and Drawings; APP_B_VIII — Identification and Control of Materials, Parts, and Components; APP_B_XIII — Handling, Storage, and Shipping

> Where multiple organizations are involved, appropriate procedures should describe interface and custody responsibilities. The identification of samples should be verified and maintained when samples are handled, transported, or transferred from one organization's responsibility to another's.

## DOC-0032-B02807

Chapter: 400 CONTROL OF SAMPLES · lines 2808–2808

Criteria: APP_B_V — Instructions, Procedures, and Drawings; APP_B_VIII — Identification and Control of Materials, Parts, and Components; APP_B_XI — Test Control; APP_B_XIII — Handling, Storage, and Shipping

> Sample characteristics, integrity, and identification should be maintained or controlled during storage. The controls applied should be consistent with the planned duration and conditions of storage and should describe action to be taken where samples have a maximum life expectancy while in storage. Storage methodology should be developed and implemented to ensure that essential sample characteristics are maintained to protect integrity. Samples should be controlled to preclude mixing of like samples. Samples on which analyses or tests have been performed should be identified and stored or disposed of as required by the procedures.
